          "text": "## 3.2. TG-DTA analysis\nThe thermal behavior ofthe obtained (Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3) CO3 carbonate precursor is analyzed using TG and DTA measurements. The TG-DTA curves for the transition-metal carbonate samples are shown in Fig. 2.\nThree endothermic peaks are observed in DSC curve, corresponding to three apparent weight loss stages in TG curve. The initial endothermic peak (1) shows up over the temperature range between 100 and 200 ◦C with a weight loss of ca. 8 wt.%, which is due to dehydration of adsorbed water. The second sharper endothermic peak (2) appears at around 250 - 365 ^∘C, accompanied by a weight loss of 26 wt.%. This peak is attributed to the thermal decomposition of the mixed carbonates. A considerable amount of CO2 is liberated continuously in this stage which constituted to the major total weight loss. Finally, the third endothermic peak (3) occurs between 370 and 400 ^∘𝖢 with a 4% weight loss. The last weight loss is mainly due to the decomposition of basic transition metal carbonate by release of structural water molecules. Only a small amount of weight loss is observed in the TG curve which becomes smooth and flat in the temperature range from 400 to\n500 ^∘C. The curve is almost flat with negligible weight loss beyond 500 ^∘𝖢, indicating the formation ofa stable complex oxide at 500 ^∘𝖢.\n(Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3) CO3",

          "text": "## 3.2. TG-DTA analysis\nThe thermal behavior ofthe obtained (Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3) CO3 carbonate precursor is analyzed using TG and DTA measurements. The TG-DTA curves for the transition-metal carbonate samples are shown in Fig. 2.\nThree endothermic peaks are observed in DSC curve, corresponding to three apparent weight loss stages in TG curve. The initial endothermic peak (1) shows up over the temperature range between 100 and 200 ◦C with a weight loss of ca. 8 wt.%, which is due to dehydration of adsorbed water. The second sharper endothermic peak (2) appears at around 250 - 365 ^∘C, accompanied by a weight loss of 26 wt.%. This peak is attributed to the thermal decomposition of the mixed carbonates. A considerable amount of CO2 is liberated continuously in this stage which constituted to the major total weight loss. Finally, the third endothermic peak (3) occurs between 370 and 400 ^∘𝖢 with a 4% weight loss. The last weight loss is mainly due to the decomposition of basic transition metal carbonate by release of structural water molecules. Only a small amount of weight loss is observed in the TG curve which becomes smooth and flat in the temperature range from 400 to\n500 ^∘C. The curve is almost flat with negligible weight loss beyond 500 ^∘𝖢, indicating the formation ofa stable complex oxide at 500 ^∘𝖢.\n(Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3) CO3",

          "text": "## 3.4. Morphology and particle size distribution\nSEM (scanning electron microscope) of the product at different magnifications is shown in Fig. 5. From these SEM micrographs, we can see that the Li (Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3) O2 powder has a uniform size distribution and regular round particle morphology. CO2 gas is released during high temperature sintering, leading to the formation of porous products with rough surface morphology and clearly visible primary particles.\nAs seen in Fig. 6, the (Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3) CO3 carbonate precursor has narrow, symmetric particle size distribution. The log-size distribution curve confirms the typical normal size distribution. The D50 of the precursor particles is around 19.765 \u0003m, while D10 and D90 are 12.244 \u0003m and 31.865 \u0003m, respectively. The distribution curves of both the precursor and the final cathode material are of similar shape. However CO2 emission during thermal decomposition results in the shrinkage of the precursor particles, leading to a smaller size cathode material. Table 3 reports the D50, D10, D90 data results of the precursor and cathode particles. The narrow particle size distribution can be obtained.\nThe SEM micrographs of Li [ (Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3)1-x Mgx ] O2 (x = 0, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05) samples at 2000 and 10,000 magnifications are shown in Fig. 7. All the samples are of round shape. The particles of Li [ (Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3)1-x Mgx ] O2 (x = 0, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05) are within a narrow size distribution range from 5 to 10 \u0003m. The surface of the powder appears as an aggregate of many small particles. Each secondary particle is composed of tightly piled cubic primary particles. The particle size decreased with the increasing Mg ^2+ content in Li [ (Ni1 / 3 Co1 / 3 Mn1 / 3)1-x Mgx ] O2 powders (x = 0, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05) The round shape becomes loose and non-uniform with increasing Mg content, indicating poor electrochemical performances ofcomposite electrode, which agreed with the following electrochemical testing results.",
